# Circadian Predictive Coding — Living Development and Research Plan

Prepared: September 24, 2026  
Repository: `OptimumAF/Circadian-Predictive-Coding`  
Inspected branch: `master`  
Inspected commit: `8793c49ee4f9f8b07649e8db6571ed53746a9a06`  
Recommended repository destination: `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`  
Status: Active implementation; Phases 0–4 and P5.1–P5.3 complete for their declared scopes. P5.4a–b now add strict typed overrides and a saved resolved-config artifact to the existing configurable continual-shift route; P5.4 parent remains open for an active-entrypoint audit. Fixed matched replay and replay side-effect ablations retain the negative circadian result. The v11 difficulty comparison is null for modulation at its fixed accuracy resolution; the fixed v12 structural comparison found no benefit from reward weighting importance history in its bounded cells. P4.8's fixed-capacity and full-stack trigger controls found inactive default adaptive sleep in their bounded cells and mixed periodic effects; no new trigger rule was selected. P4.9 records the NumPy/Torch capability boundary and hash-bound NumPy-only v14 provenance. P5.1 adds an opt-in versioned execution manifest without changing fixed v14 result bytes. P5.2 projects observed v14 streams and separately versioned genuine wake diagnostics after completed scoring. P5.3 stages and atomically publishes validated artifacts and resumes only checked unscored trial prefixes; P5.4 parent and P5.5–P5.7 remain open. P2.6a deeper attribution remains deferred.

## Purpose and review boundary

Continue the existing project rather than rebuilding it. Turn its wake/sleep, chemical-gating, replay, and structural-plasticity mechanisms into a well-specified, reproducible research system. Determine where circadian predictive coding actually helps, what causes any benefit, and what accuracy, forgetting, compute, and memory tradeoffs it introduces.

This plan is based on a static review of repository documentation, the NumPy predictive-coding and circadian implementations, the Torch head implementation, benchmark orchestration, continual-shift profiles, CI configuration, and selected tests. No local test suite or training run was completed during preparation: fetching a local clone was blocked by network name resolution. Findings below distinguish observed code behavior from questions requiring tests. Re-audit the current checkout before implementation; the repository may have changed since the inspected commit.

The project is an algorithmic learning research project. “Circadian” describes its wake/sleep-inspired control scheme; it is not evidence of a biological circadian oscillator or a clinical circadian-prediction model. The current “reward” mechanism is supervised batch-difficulty scaling, not an environmental reinforcement-learning reward.

## 1. Mandatory instructions for the implementing agent

### 1.1 Treat this document as a living execution record

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan, relevant ADRs, and the latest work log at the start of every session. Preserve existing repository instructions and architectural conventions. Add a brief pointer to this plan in `AGENTS.md` rather than replacing that file.

**Update this plan continuously as you work. Mark a task `[x]` only after implementing or investigating it, satisfying its acceptance criteria, and recording concrete evidence. Do not wait until the whole project is finished to update checkboxes.**

Keep stable task IDs. Use:

- `[ ]` for work not completed.
- `[ ] ... — IN PROGRESS` for the current task.
- `[ ] ... — BLOCKED: reason; unblock condition` for blocked work.
- `[x] ... — evidence: test/log/report/commit reference` for verified completion.

A deferred, superseded, or canceled task is not a successfully completed implementation. Keep it unchecked, add the explicit disposition, preserve the reason in the change log, and exclude it transparently from active-scope progress calculations. If a previously completed task regresses, reopen it and record the regression.

### 1.2 Adjust the plan when evidence justifies it

You may split, reorder, add, or replace tasks as repository inspection and experiments reveal better approaches. Make the amendment before implementing the changed scope. Record the original assumption, new evidence, rationale, affected tasks, dependencies, and acceptance criteria. Do not silently delete difficult tasks or weaken acceptance criteria to make the plan appear complete.

A research task can be completed with a negative result. “No improvement detected,” “hypothesis rejected,” and “uncertain within this budget” are valid outcomes when the protocol was followed and evidence is preserved. Never manipulate baselines, seeds, metrics, or stopping conditions to force circadian models to win.

### 1.3 Session workflow

Inspect the working tree and preserve unrelated user changes. Identify the smallest unblocked task on the critical path, inspect its implementation and tests, write or update a regression test when practical, implement the smallest coherent change, and run the relevant checks. Update the task, supporting documentation, and work log in the same logical change. Continue to the next unblocked task without repeatedly asking for permission for ordinary implementation decisions within scope.

Pause before destructive actions, publishing, paid infrastructure, new external data uploads, or material changes to the project's scientific objective. Keep experiments local by default. Do not introduce remote experiment tracking, cloud dependencies, or automatic telemetry merely for convenience.

If execution ends mid-task, leave it unchecked and record the exact next command or coding step. Do not claim background execution or an experiment result that has not been observed.

### 1.4 Evidence and completion contract

For each nontrivial task, record the behavior changed, files touched, exact commands, exit status, tests passed/failed/skipped, relevant artifact paths, and remaining limitations. For experiments, additionally record the protocol, configurations, seeds, hardware, failures, and decision. Code existing in the repository is not sufficient evidence of correctness; a passing test suite that skipped the relevant backend is not sufficient backend validation.

Suggested task record:

```markdown
### P1.1 — Separate validation from final testing
Status: IN PROGRESS
Depends on: P0.1, P0.2
Acceptance: training, rollback, scheduling, and model selection cannot access test labels.
Evidence: pending
Changed files: pending
Validation commands and outcomes: pending
Limitations or blockers: none identified
Next action: introduce split-role-aware dataset containers and leakage regression tests.
```

Maintain `docs/development-log.md` for evidence and `docs/adr/` for scientific or architectural decisions. Keep the current task and next action in the handoff section at the end of this document.

## 2. Existing foundation and observed issues

### Preserve and extend

The inspected repository already includes NumPy backpropagation, predictive-coding, and circadian networks; Torch ResNet-50 reference models with predictive-coding heads; chemical-gated plasticity; adaptive sleep triggers and budgets; structural split/prune logic; homeostasis; NumPy replay; rollback handling; continual-shift and multi-seed benchmarks; figures and a dashboard; and tests for several important mechanisms.

Do not create replacement implementations of replay, adaptation policies, adaptive scheduling, or function-preserving splitting without first evaluating and extending the existing implementation. In particular, `NeuronAdaptationPolicy` is already used by the NumPy sleep path; do not treat it as an unused abstraction.

### Priority findings from the inspected code

| ID | Observed behavior or verification target | Consequence for the plan |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | `src/app/resnet50_benchmark.py` uses `loaders.test_loader` for early-stop decisions and pre/post-sleep rollback metrics. | Separate training, validation/guard data, and final testing before interpreting new results as held-out evidence. |
| F2 | Backprop uses a linear classifier; predictive/circadian variants use hidden-layer heads. Backprop can train its backbone, while the PC variants are instantiated with frozen backbones. | Add an architecture- and representation-matched benchmark track. Preserve end-to-end backprop as a separately labeled practical reference. |
| F3 | Benchmark models run sequentially after a shared seed setup and reuse loader objects. Model-specific seeds also differ. | Verify and control initialization, backbone weights, sample order, augmentation, and RNG streams rather than assuming a common numeric seed makes runs matched. |
| F4 | The NumPy sleep method returns before homeostasis and replay when both structural budgets are zero. | Make consolidation independently executable so replay-only and no-structure ablations are meaningful. |
| F5 | Multilayer ordinary PC relaxes multiple latent states; circadian PC relaxes its final adaptive state and propagates updates through preceding layers. | Document the distinction and implement a truly matched comparison before attributing a multilayer result to circadian control alone. |
| F6 | NumPy and Torch log differently defined energy quantities, and the Torch logged squared-error quantity is not simply the cross-entropy objective suggested by its update. | Specify objectives and gradients; distinguish optimized energy from diagnostic scores and width-dependent normalizations. |
| F7 | Prediction methods in the reviewed PC paths are feedforward; iterative latent relaxation is used during training. | Treat reduced relaxation steps as a training optimization. Do not promise an existing iterative serving-latency reduction. |
| F8 | CI installs the base requirements, which omit Torch; the Torch test file uses `pytest.importorskip("torch")`. | Add a guaranteed Torch CPU test job and report skips explicitly. Existing CI configuration is not proof that Torch tests ran. |

These observations identify what must be fixed or verified. They do not quantify how much historical rankings would change. Preserve historical outputs and their provenance rather than rewriting or deleting them.

Repository evidence is indexed in Section 15.

---

## 3. Research objective and scope

The first defensible milestone is a reproducible answer to:

> Under controlled representations, data access, capacity, memory, and compute budgets, when do chemical gating, sleep consolidation, and structural adaptation improve retention or adaptation relative to ordinary predictive coding and backpropagation?

Predefine four primary hypotheses:

**H1 — Plasticity control.** Chemical gating can improve retention/adaptation tradeoffs compared with otherwise identical ordinary predictive coding.

**H2 — Structural adaptation.** Split/prune decisions can improve the accuracy–capacity or accuracy–compute tradeoff beyond fixed-width and simple scheduled-growth controls.

**H3 — Consolidation.** Replay and homeostasis contribute independently of extra data exposure and training updates. Compare against replay-equipped PC and backprop, not only no-replay baselines.

**H4 — Scheduling.** Adaptive sleep decisions offer a better tradeoff than periodic sleep under a declared sleep/compute budget.

Success does not require winning every benchmark. A useful outcome is a stable implementation and a clear account of where each hypothesis is supported, contradicted, or unresolved.

Near-term scope: correctness, matched benchmarks, transactional sleep, bounded replay, reliable experiment artifacts, controlled continual-learning studies, and measured performance improvements. Defer direct ResNet convolutional rewiring, large generative replay systems, distributed training, and broad architecture searches until smaller experiments justify them.

**Exit gate:** Every claimed mechanism has an independent switch and a matched control. Buffer size, replay exposure, information access, and effective update counts are observable. Added policy complexity is justified by an ablation or remains experimental.

Suggested output layout, to be created by implementation rather than assumed to exist:

```text
artifacts/<run-id>/
  manifest.json
  resolved-config.json
  metrics.jsonl
  sleep-events.jsonl
  topology.jsonl
  checkpoints/
  seed-results.json
  summary.json
  report.md
```

**Exit gate:** A fresh invocation can reproduce a small documented run and regenerate its report. A stopped run resumes correctly or fails with a precise incompatibility error. Missing provenance is visible, not silently invented.

## 10. Phase 6 — Run controlled ablations and broaden continual-learning evidence

**Dependencies:** P1–P5.  
**Goal:** Answer the hypotheses with measured uncertainty, not a single best run.

### 10.1 Stage experiments by cost

- [ ] **P6.1 — Smoke suite.** Use tiny deterministic synthetic fixtures to test every enabled branch and all artifact outputs. Include deliberately forced sleep events and a stationary control. Smoke results establish functionality only.
- [ ] **P6.2 — Reproduce corrected toy profiles.** Run the existing baseline, strength-case, and hardest-case scenarios under the new protocol. Preserve their original tuning history. Report changes caused by corrected mathematics/protocol separately from new policy improvements.
- [ ] **P6.3 — Run a staged mechanism matrix.** Begin with a few development seeds and a small number of factors. Establish a fixed budget before expanding. Reserve independent confirmation seeds/configurations and final test sets before selecting winners.
- [ ] **P6.4 — Extend the stream protocol.** Add A→B→A recurrence and longer A→B→C sequences, stationary noise, abrupt/gradual drift, and low-data adaptation. Log phase/task information available to each method; future task boundaries must not be an undeclared advantage.
- [ ] **P6.5 — Add a modest real-data continual task.** After multiclass/data-contract readiness, select a small split or transformed-image benchmark. State whether the task is domain-incremental, task-incremental, or class-incremental; use the correct output/head and task-ID assumptions.
- [ ] **P6.6 — Run frozen-feature vision comparisons.** Start with a small controlled dataset/subset and shared representation. Expand to CIFAR-100 only after smoke, matched-head, and protocol checks pass. Keep end-to-end backprop separate.
- [ ] **P6.7 — Confirm promising or informative results.** For inexpensive experiments, plan roughly ten independent confirmation seeds when feasible; use pilot variability to justify final sample size. Smaller GPU studies must be labeled exploratory when uncertainty remains large. Do not stop a seed sweep when rankings become favorable.

### 10.2 Minimum comparison matrix

| Variant | What it isolates |
|---|---|
| Matched backprop head | Learning-rule reference with the same representation and head shape. |
| Ordinary PC | Circadian control reference under the specified PC formulation. |
| PC + chemical gating only | Plasticity gating without sleep or extra replay work. |
| PC + replay, no structural changes | Consolidation without growth/pruning. |
| PC + periodic structure, no replay | Structural control under a simple schedule. |
| PC + homeostasis/reset, fixed width | Nonstructural sleep effects. |
| Full circadian | Combined mechanism under recorded costs. |
| Full circadian minus one component | Contribution of replay, gating, structure, scheduling, or difficulty modulation. |
| Backprop + matched replay | Whether memory access explains retention improvements. |
| Wider fixed PC/backprop | Whether more capacity alone explains the result. |
| Scheduled/random growth control | Whether usage-aware selection beats simpler capacity allocation. |

A fixed-width model chosen after observing the circadian model's final width is a retrospective capacity/oracle reference. Label it accordingly; do not present it as a fair online deployment policy or as equal total training compute. Include planned-width references as well.

### 10.3 Metrics and statistical analysis

- [ ] **P6.8 — Predeclare primary metrics.** Use final average task accuracy and forgetting/retention for continual learning, with adaptation speed or accuracy-over-stream as secondary outcomes. Retain the existing balanced score as a secondary diagnostic rather than the sole ranking criterion.
- [ ] **P6.9 — Record the accuracy matrix.** Let `A[t,j]` be held-out accuracy on task `j` after stage `t`. Compute retention and forgetting from explicit stored values; handle undefined denominators and negative/positive transfer transparently. Evaluate final tests without feeding results back into model selection.
- [ ] **P6.10 — Measure resource costs.** Report wall time, wake updates, latent iterations, replay exposure, sleep/guard overhead, peak memory, replay bytes, initial/final/peak parameters, and parameter history. Show accuracy/forgetting against compute and memory instead of a single composite winner.
- [ ] **P6.11 — Use seed-level paired analysis.** Report individual seeds, mean, dispersion, confidence intervals, and paired differences on shared data/split seeds. Treat runs/seeds—not individual minibatches—as the replication unit. Predeclare primary contrasts and avoid selective multiple-comparison reporting.
- [ ] **P6.12 — Publish failures and null findings in the report.** Preserve divergence, timeouts, rejected sleeps, and regressions. Explain whether a hypothesis is supported, rejected, or unresolved within the measured setting. Distinguish tuning results from independent confirmation.

**Exit gate:** Each primary hypothesis has a reproducible result or a clearly documented remaining uncertainty. No ranking depends on test-informed tuning, unmatched replay privileges, undisclosed capacity growth, or cherry-picked seeds.

---

## 11. Phase 7 — Optimize measured bottlenecks

**Dependencies:** A correct, reproducible baseline and Phase 6 pilot measurements.

- [ ] **P7.1 — Profile component costs.** Separate data loading/transfers, backbone features, latent relaxation, weight updates, replay, sleep mutation, guard evaluation, and artifact writing. Keep end-to-end and kernel/step timings distinct.
- [ ] **P7.2 — Standardize performance measurement.** Preserve appropriate device synchronization, warmups, sample counts, batch-size reporting, and actual measured batch counts. Report p50/p95 serving latency and throughput with timing scope. Do not derive throughput from a requested batch count when fewer batches ran.
- [ ] **P7.3 — Optimize training-time latent relaxation.** Compare fixed iteration counts with bounded residual-based early stopping and, later, learned initialization. Enforce minimum/maximum steps and report convergence failures. Verify the accuracy–training-cost tradeoff on confirmation runs.
- [ ] **P7.4 — Evaluate frozen-feature caching.** Cache only when representation and preprocessing semantics are fixed. Hash backbone state, preprocessing, dataset identity, and augmentation policy. Do not silently replace an augmented online benchmark with one cached view per image.
- [ ] **P7.5 — Reduce avoidable synchronization and allocation.** Optimize only measured hotspots. Preserve a simple reference implementation and numeric comparison tests. Check that dynamic tensor changes do not leave stale optimizer references.
- [ ] **P7.6 — Evaluate precision/compilation carefully.** Add mixed precision or compilation only where compatible with manual updates and dynamic shapes. Keep a float64/float32 reference for validation, log precision, and measure recompilation/shape-change overhead.
- [ ] **P7.7 — Test inference/export after adaptation.** Load adapted checkpoints and verify feedforward predictions, stable shapes, and latency. A serving path must not require targets, replay buffers, or wake-learning state updates.

**Exit gate:** Optimizations improve the measured accuracy–cost tradeoff or are rejected with evidence. Current feedforward serving is not mislabeled as iterative predictive-coding inference.

---

## 12. Phase 8 — Gated extensions: deeper plasticity and broader scientific questions

**Dependencies:** P2's matched multilayer formulation, P3's structural invariants, and P6's evidence.  
**Status:** Deferred research scope until entry gates pass; not automatically required for the first reliable release.

- [ ] **P8.1 — Write an extension decision record.** Identify what shallow models cannot answer, the expected benefit, the budget, and the disconfirming outcome. Prefer a small multilayer MLP before structural edits inside ResNet blocks.
- [ ] **P8.2 — Generalize layer state and identity.** Associate chemical, traffic, importance, age, cooldown, and adaptation policy state with each eligible layer. Preserve tensor-neighbor relationships and stable IDs without a wholesale rewrite of unrelated code.
- [ ] **P8.3 — Implement one additional adaptive layer.** Update incoming weights, biases, outgoing weights, state arrays, and optimizer/checkpoint representation consistently. Start without normalization/residual complications; add those only in a separately specified experiment.
- [ ] **P8.4 — Validate deeper function preservation and rollback.** Test repeated splits/prunes, neighbor shapes, prediction preservation, full-state restoration, and resume. Compare fixed, final-layer-adaptive, and multi-layer-adaptive models under declared budgets.
- [ ] **P8.5 — Evaluate whether additional biological-inspired controls are useful.** A true phase oscillator or entrainment rule is optional research, not a prerequisite and not already established by the name. Introduce it only with a measurable question and an ablation against the simpler scheduler.

**Exit gate:** A small deeper-plasticity experiment is reproducible and better answers a stated question. No full-network structural system is promoted merely because it is more elaborate.

---

## 13. Phase 9 — Continuous validation, documentation, and release candidate

**Dependencies:** Relevant earlier phases. CI hardening should start during P0/P1, not wait until the end.

- [ ] **P9.1 — Require Torch CPU coverage.** Keep the lightweight NumPy job and add a guaranteed Torch/torchvision CPU job. Make unexpected skips visible or failing for required suites. Use synthetic fixtures or mocked feature extractors to avoid network downloads in routine tests. — IN PROGRESS: CPU job added and locally validated; first actual CI execution remains unverified. See `docs/development-log.md`.
- [ ] **P9.2 — Expand regression coverage.** Include leakage prevention, matched initialization, model-order invariance, math checks, zero-structure sleep, topology invariants, replay bounds, full rollback/resume, config serialization, and artifact validation. Add deterministic randomized/property-style cases where useful.
- [ ] **P9.3 — Establish reproducible dependencies.** Keep broad supported ranges where appropriate, but save tested environment versions or lock/constraints files for published experiments. Avoid unreviewed framework upgrades during a benchmark campaign.
- [ ] **P9.4 — Update documentation to actual behavior.** Explain the learning equations, biological-inspired terminology, algorithm variants, benchmark tracks, dataset information access, resource accounting, and backend capability differences. Preserve compatibility notes and migration guidance.
- [ ] **P9.5 — Regenerate research reports and figures.** Generate from validated artifacts, showing uncertainty, compute/memory costs, negative results, and limitations. Label historical figures and do not imply they were reproduced on the new protocol.
- [ ] **P9.6 — Validate installation and examples.** Test clean Python environments, NumPy-only usage, Torch CPU usage, and the documented Windows/PowerShell workflow where available. Capture unavailable-platform testing honestly.
- [ ] **P9.7 — Prepare a release candidate.** Update `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/model-card.md`, architecture docs, ADRs, and release instructions. Include a small reproducibility bundle and exact commands. Do not publish releases, push changes, or deploy Pages without the relevant authorization.

**First reliable release definition:** A clean environment passes required non-skipped tests; corrected matched benchmarks are reproducible; all test access is isolated; math and sleep contracts are documented/tested; checkpoint/resume works; ablation artifacts show real outcomes; and limitations are explicit. Universal superiority and completion of optional deep-plasticity research are not release requirements.

---

## 14. Recommended implementation order and scope controls

Use small reviewable changes, each with its own tests and updated plan status:

1. **Baseline and tracking:** P0 plus the minimal Torch CI coverage improvement.
2. **Evaluation isolation:** P1.1–P1.3 and protocol-version plumbing.
3. **Matched baselines and RNGs:** P1.4–P1.8, including model-order invariance.
4. **Mathematical specification and fixes:** P2; keep legacy variants named and reproducible.
5. **Sleep transaction and component isolation:** P3 with full rollback and zero-structure tests.
6. **Replay controls and experiment artifacts:** P4 and P5, staged rather than one large framework rewrite.
7. **First controlled study:** Phase 6 synthetic ablations and the hypothesis report.
8. **Vision, optimization, and optional depth:** Expand only after the preceding evidence supports the added cost.

Do not combine a benchmark protocol fix, model-math change, and policy improvement into one uninterpretable comparison. When they must land near each other, retain intermediate runs or fixtures so the cause of changed behavior is traceable.

Default compute policy: start on CPU with tiny models; detect actual GPU and memory instead of assuming hardware; progress to small GPU runs; estimate sweep cost; enforce budgets; preserve failures; and reserve long confirmation runs for explicit documented candidates. Proposed future config/runner interfaces are not executable commands until implemented and documented.

### Definition of done for an individual coding task

The intended behavior is implemented, relevant tests are added, required checks pass without hiding relevant skips, documentation/configuration is updated, and the evidence log points to exact commands and outputs. An unavailable GPU benchmark remains unverified rather than being inferred from a CPU test.

### Definition of done for an individual experiment

The preregistered local protocol and budgets were followed, all intended seeds and failures are accounted for, raw outputs and metadata are saved, analysis is reproducible, and the hypothesis decision is written. The result may be positive, negative, or inconclusive.

### End-of-session update template

```markdown
Date:
Repository commit and working-tree state:
Tasks completed, with evidence:
Task still in progress:
Tests run; passed/failed/skipped:
Experiments and artifact paths:
Plan amendments and rationale:
Blockers and unblock conditions:
Next exact action:
```

**Final instruction to the agent:** Execute incrementally, keep this plan current, mark work complete only with evidence, and adjust the remaining plan whenever validated findings justify a better path. Preserve the scientific question even when the original hypothesis does not win.
